## 2026-09-20 w9/dtn-e2e

CHANGE w9/dtn-e2e (branch `w9/dtn-e2e` from dev `52eb0db`, merged forward to `d50c392`, worktree `build/lanes/w9-dtn-e2e`, commit `8fac047`): **`books/tcpcl-session` exports two new recognizers and nine entry points change their guard.** `fn-tcl-inbound-cheapp (i limit)` and `fn-tcl-session-cheapp (s)` are `fn-tcl-inboundp` and `fn-tcl-sessionp` with the two conjuncts that walk the staged inbound segments removed (`fn-tcl-octet-listsp` and the `fn-tcl-lists-len` equation). The guard of `fn-tcl-drive`, `-step`, `-decode-for`, `-recv-segment`, `-recv-ack`, `-recv-refuse`, `-pump`, `-send` and `-tick`, and the `:logic` side of `fn-tcl-drive`'s totality `mbe`, is now `fn-tcl-session-cheapp`. **No keystone statement changed**: C1 to C4 and every `-preserves-sessionp` lemma still hypothesise `fn-tcl-sessionp`, and the enabled rewrite `fn-tcl-sessionp-is-cheap` (with `fn-tcl-inboundp-is-cheap`) discharges the weaker one, so an includer needs no edit; if a proof of yours splits on the new `mbe` test, that rule closes it. Also: `fn-tcl-lists-len` and `fn-tcl-concat-rev` are now `:guard t` (the latter's body is `fn-tcl-app`, which `fn-tcl-app-is-append` rewrites to `append`, so its logical value is unchanged), and `fn-tcl-app` moved above `fn-tcl-concat-rev`. New preservation lemmas, one per transition, `fn-tcl-*-preserves-cheapp`, plus `fn-tcl-initial-session-is-cheap`. Certification on hbox is in flight at the time of writing (`run-20260920T180202Z-01c4`, `/tank/fn/lanes/w9-dtn-e2e`); the verdict follows in a NOTE.

NOTE w9/dtn-e2e -> everyone who writes a guard: **an `mbe` with `:exec nil` does not make a served path cheap; the guard does.** `specs/tcpcl.md` §3 claimed "no recognizer runs over retained input on the served path (`fn-tcl-drive`'s `mbe` check is `:exec nil`)" and that was wrong, which is how `fn-tcl-drive` shipped with an O(n^2/chunk) cost that w8 measured and could not close. The native host calls into the core through `fnn-call` (`host/native/io.lisp:11-18`), which applies the ACL2 *executable counterpart*, and an executable counterpart checks its callee's guard on **every** call — so a whole-state recognizer in a guard is re-run per chunk however free the body is. If your book has a served path, the thing to make cheap is the `:guard`, and the thing that makes a cheap guard sound is a preservation lemma per transition plus one establishing lemma at the initial state.

## 2026-09-20 w5/host-cleanup

CHANGE w5/host-cleanup (branch `w5/host-cleanup` from dev `1c5b950`, worktree `build/lanes/w5-host-cleanup`, handoff `planning/lanes/HANDOFF-w5-host-cleanup.md`): **every host file now `ld`s or includes what it uses, so no bridge's `ld` order is load-bearing any more.** `lints.host_names` **81 -> 0**, no name excused and no allowlist entry for a finding. `host/store-node-host.lisp` and `host/config-host.lisp` `(ld "store-host.lisp")`; `host/checkpoint-host.lisp`, `host/bp-ingress-host.lisp` and `host/owner-host.lisp` `(ld "store-node-host.lisp")`; `host/native/reader-model-host.lisp` `(ld "../reader-host.lisp")`; `host/simulator.lisp` includes `books/acceptance` and `host/workflow-host.lisp` includes `books/store-node`. **Nothing was copied and nothing moved**: a second `ld` of a file already in the session re-admits identical definitions, which ACL2 accepts as redundant, and an `ld` inside a host file resolves against that file's own directory. **The raw pair was circular** -- `host/native/io.lisp` called `fnn-dispatch-tcpcl` and `host/native/tcpcl.lisp` called twenty-four io.lisp entry points -- so io.lisp now has a three-function verb registry (`*fnn-verbs*`, `fnn-register-verb`, `fnn-verb-handler`), names nothing in the layer above it, and answers an unregistered verb as unknown; tcpcl.lisp registers `"tcpcl"` at its end and loads io.lisp itself when `fnn-core` is unbound. Edit for a bridge: none required. A bridge may now drop the sibling `ld`s it issues before the file it wants, and a new one need not know the order at all.

CHANGE w5/host-cleanup -> tooling: **`tools/host_check.py`, run by `make check` when `FN_ACL2` is set** (loudly skipped otherwise; a skipped run is not evidence). One fresh ACL2 per host file, that file and nothing else; the three kinds are read off `host/native/build.lisp` rather than typed (`ld` for the wrappers, `raw` for what build.lisp `load`s under `set-raw-mode`, build.lisp itself skipped as the image script). On persvati (acl2-8.7, `/home/ember/fn-lanes/w5-host-cleanup`, certificates from `/home/ember/fn-certcache`): **21/21 load alone**. Two `tools/ledger.py` defects fixed rather than worked around: `slot_accessors` compared a `defstruct` option head to `"conc-name"` without stripping the keyword colon (so all thirteen accessors of `(defstruct (fnn-tcl-conn (:conc-name fnn-tclc-)) ...)` were reported undefined), and `load` is now read as the raw-mode load edge `ld` is for the rest. `tools/acl2-builtins.txt` gained `cddr`, `max` and `close` -- real ACL2/CL names beside the `cdddr`/`cddddr` already there, by that file's own documented procedure, not exemptions. Caveat: `make test` runs `check` before `certify`, so with `FN_ACL2` set and no certificates installed the host check fails there.

NOTE w5/host-cleanup: **a failed `ld` does not end an ACL2 session reading from a pipe** -- ACL2 prints the error, abandons that form and reads the next one. The first version of `tools/host_check.py` therefore reported `ok` for `host/config-host.lisp` with its one `ld` edge deleted: the transcript held `ACL2 Error [Translate] in ( DEFUN FN-CFG-HOST-INITIAL-OCTETS ...)` and then the success marker. Any tool that drives ACL2 on stdin and reads a marker to mean success has this bug. The fix, and the shape to copy: put the load inside an `er-progn` with the form that prints the marker, so it short-circuits, and fail on any `ACL2 Error` printed before that marker. The second marker, a separate top-level form afterwards, is how the `ACL2 !>` prompt is checked -- a file left in `(program)` mode prints `ACL2 p>` there, which is the anchor-host `(logic)` rule made into a test. Re-measured on the same deletion: FAIL, exit 1.

NOTE w5/host-cleanup -> store, checkpoint: **`tests/test_checkpoint.py` had not run on any recent tree and nothing noticed**, the third instance of the defect class w5/scale-2 named for `tests/bench/generate.py`. All six tests died in `post_many` at `run_store.group_codes(["fn.letters"], store.config)`; `group_codes` takes the `Store` (it reads `store.config_domain`, the allocation domain the core hands it at recover). One line past that, `assert bridge.existing_action(...) == "new"` pins a name the model does not use: `fn-store-sn-existing-action` answers `:absent` when nothing is stored under the identity, and `run_store.post` refuses on `duplicate` and `conflict` and proceeds otherwise. Both repaired, no production code touched; `tests.test_checkpoint` is **6/6 in 480.9 s** on persvati, `test_process_death_at_every_cut_recovers_old_authority_or_complete_generation` included -- it had never run, so the "every crash point" evidence for checkpoints dates from this run and not before it. Whole set for this lane (`test_store_lifecycle reader post owner bp_receive checkpoint workflow_journal`): 73 tests, 893.9 s, and the one remaining failure is the board-diagnosed one-durable-post-per-connection case in `test_post`, left failing and untouched.
## 2026-09-20 w9/reconfig

CHANGE w9/reconfig M1 (branch `w9/reconfig` from dev `52eb0db`, worktree `build/lanes/w9-reconfig`, commits `7f59156`, `c914216`): **the two-kind on-disk interleaving is DECIDED, and the decision is a sequence field, not a file format.** The layout stays two directories -- article records in the transaction journal, configuration records under `config/` -- and the STREAM is one: every record of either kind carries its position in the unified stream in its own sequence field, and recovery merges the two files by that field before it replays anything. New book `books/config-stream.lisp`, prefix `fn-cstr-` (registered in `docs/prefixes.md`), certified on persvati (ACL2 8.7, evidence `build/acl2/certify-20260920T181223Z-1585622`; tests `-181256Z-1591093`). Keystones: `fn-cstr-merge-keeps-every-config-record`, `fn-cstr-merge-keeps-every-article-record`, `fn-cstr-merge-is-sequence-ordered`, `fn-cstr-ok-merged-replay-ends-in-a-configured-node`, `fn-cstr-created-group-is-served-exactly-from-its-generation`, `fn-cstr-retired-group-is-served-exactly-below-its-generation`. `books/node-config` is untouched and `fn-cnode-replay-loop` with its fold keystone is unchanged: the merge feeds that loop. **For the store cluster**: `fn-cfg-record-make`'s first argument stops being the previous generation and becomes the journal position, which is what `fn-ocfg-reconfig-record` already passes and what `host/store-node-host.lisp:124` still does not; changing it is the one host edit that makes `fn-cstr-replay` usable at open. `tests/acl2/config-stream-tests.lisp` holds the separating witness that says why: a `(:set-capacity 1)` over a history whose two articles reserve 2 is `:ok` to the configuration-only replay and `:config-refusal` to the merged one.

CHANGE w9/reconfig M2 (commit `b08edbe`, affects `host/store-node-host.lisp`, `tools/run_store.py`, `bin/fn`): **`fn-store-cfg-reconfigure` takes a number.** Signature is now `(kind name-octets n monotonic wall state)` and it accepts `:set-capacity`, building `(fn-cfg-set-capacity (nfix n))` and admitting it through the same `fn-cnode-record-acceptablep` replay applies, against the live node's reservation total. Callers: `Acl2Store.reconfigure(kind, name, monotonic, wall, n=0)`. New CLI `fn store capacity <n>` / `run_store.py capacity <n>`; a DECREASE is additionally gated, before anything becomes durable, by replaying the candidate configuration history against the store's real article records in a second core (`_candidate_history_opens`), because configuration records are not yet interleaved on disk and a decrease would otherwise be applied to the whole article history at open. `*fn-store-capacity*` is gone from `fn-store-sn-reset`, whose pre-open state now has capacity 0 (section 1.6's fail-closed floor); it survives only in `host/checkpoint-host.lisp`, which is not this lane's file. Not run here: the Python suite needs a local ACL2 bridge and this lane runs none.

CHANGE w9/reconfig M3 (commit `7f59156`, new book `books/owner-config.lisp`, prefix `fn-ocfg-`): **the owner event `(:reconfigure id deltas)` and the per-connection pin, as a layer, not a slot.** `books/owner.lisp` and `books/owner-invariants.lisp` are UNTOUCHED -- `fn-ocfg-make (owner config pins staged)` does to `books/owner` what `books/node-config` did to `books/node`. The board's `w5-config-groups -> w5/owner-post` proposal is implemented with three deviations, all forced. (1) The pin is a table beside the owner, not a ninth slot of `fn-own-conn-make`: the slot rewrites forty call sites in a file two lanes are editing this cycle, and `fn-ocfg-statep` holds the table's domain equal to the open connections, which is what makes the pin derived rather than writable. (2) The proposal's name `fn-own-conn-config` is TAKEN -- it is the posting configuration (`fn-inj-configp`, `books/injection`) pinned at open -- so the reconfiguration pin is `fn-ocfg-conn-config`. (3) The record's sequence is `(len (fn-sf-records ...))`, the unified-stream position, per M1. Keystones stated: `fn-ocfg-reconfiguration-never-changes-what-an-open-connection-serves`, `fn-ocfg-pin-is-stable-without-advance`, `fn-ocfg-open-pins-the-live-configuration`, `fn-ocfg-advance-observes-the-live-configuration`, `fn-ocfg-list-active-lists-the-pinned-served-table`. **No event is claimed**: `books/owner` has no certificate on any box, so nothing that includes it certifies.

ASK w9/reconfig -> the NNTP cluster (the R5 wire seam, `books/nntp-responses.lisp:225`, `:308`, `:319`): **`LIST ACTIVE` still lists the allocation domain, and one argument fixes it.** `fn-nntp-list-active` already takes its group list as a parameter; the three call sites in `fn-nntp-dispatch` supply `(fn-state-groups archive)`, which since packet R3 is the allocation DOMAIN -- every name the configuration history ever created, retired ones included. The pinned served table is `fn-cfg-group-names` at the connection's generation. The change is one served-table argument on `fn-served-conn` (`books/served.lisp`), `fn-served-dispatch` and `fn-nntp-dispatch`, filled from `fn-ocfg-conn-config` at the owner. Until it lands, `fn-ocfg-list-active` is a sibling API and `books/owner-config.lisp` says so in a comment where the equating theorem would go, rather than stating a theorem about a function the host does not call.

PROPOSAL w9/reconfig -> the substrate lane (R8, propagation between peers): **a proposed group is NOT a configuration record, and keeping it out is the guarantee.** The requirement's core is already proved: `fn-cnode-article-transitions-never-change-config` (`books/node-config.lisp:331`) says every article and ingest transition leaves `fn-cnode-config` equal, so nothing a peer sends can move B's served table; the only transition that moves it is `fn-cnode-apply-config` on a record B's own `fn-cnode-record-acceptablep` admits. If a `:proposed-group` were a delta kind, that theorem would have to be reproved and would be weaker. So the proposal should arrive as a `:policy` statement (yours) or a control-channel notice, carrying the proposing peer's identity and the name, which a LOCAL admissibility rule may consult and which an operator answers with `fn group create`. The two-node harness scenario (create on A, propose to B, admit on B, post reaches B's new group) is not in `tools/twonode_gate.py` and this lane did not add it: `fn group create` refuses while an owner is live (the writer lock), so the scenario needs `(:reconfigure ...)` wired into `host/owner-host.lisp` first.

PROPOSAL w9/reconfig -> the BP/scheduler lane (R7, contact plans as configuration records): the configuration value has no contact slot and neither does `fn-cfg-peerp`, so this lane did not write one while `books/config` and `books/peer-config` are both being certified elsewhere. The shape: two delta kinds `:set-contact name rows` / `:remove-contact name` writing rows keyed by peer name and slot-labelled `"contact"` into the peers slot, with a slot-aware `fn-cfg-rows-without-key` variant so a contact upsert does not clobber the peer's transport rows; and a reader book `books/contact-config.lisp` deriving `fn-sched-contact peer start end` (`books/scheduler.lisp:66`) from the configuration generation, keystone: a contact read from an admissible configuration is a `fn-sched-contactp`, every scheduler keystone unchanged and recertified.
## 2026-09-20 w5/clock-seam

CHANGE w5/clock-seam (branch `w5/clock-seam` from dev `1c5b950`, worktree `build/lanes/w5-clock-seam`): **the injection clock is per submission, not per connection.** This closes the owner-followups NOTE ("one pinned observation per connection means ONE durable post per connection"). RFC 5537 3.4 makes `Injection-Date` the time of injection and `books/injection.lisp` derives a generated `Message-ID` from the same reading, so one reading pinned at accept gave every submission on a connection the identity of the first and the host refused the second as a duplicate. **Two readings now, with distinct jobs**: `observation`, pinned at accept, unchanged, is the READER environment (`fn-nntp-env`: DATE, NEWGROUPS); `injection`, supplied with this wire event, is the ONLY one `fn-inj-decide` sees. Edits for an includer: `fn-nntp-post-step (ps archive config observation injection wire-event)` -- the new argument is FIFTH, before `wire-event`; the served connection is SIX fields with `fn-served-conn-injection` at index 5, so `fn-served-make-conn (wire session archive config observation injection)` and `fn-served-open (archive line-limit body-limit config observation injection)`; `fn-served-dispatch`, `fn-served-feed`, `fn-served-step` and `fn-served-run` keep their signatures and every one of their keystone statements is unchanged, because the reading rides in the record. `books/owner.lisp` rebuilds the served connection on every read and puts `(fn-own-clock o)` -- its current observation, the one the host last reported through `(:observe obs)` -- in that field (`fn-own-read`, `fn-own-read-step`, `fn-own-outcome`, `fn-own-open`); `tools/run_owner.py` takes a reading in `serve()` before each socket chunk. **Statements changed, minimally**: the two K1 keystones `fn-own-read-is-served-step-on-pinned-prefix` and `fn-own-reader-sees-pinned-prefix-replay` (with their `-after-any-trace` forms) thread `(fn-own-clock o)` / `(fn-own-clock final)` as the sixth argument of `fn-served-make-conn`; `fn-own-open-session-boundedp` and every `fn-nntp-post-step` theorem gain the argument; `fn-post-refused-body-submits-nothing`'s hypothesis is now about the injection clock, which is the clock the refusal is a function of. No conclusion anywhere was weakened or removed. **NOT taken at `fn-own-take-submission`** as proposed: the decision, the identity and the 441 for a refused body are all made when the body arrives inside `fn-nntp-post-step`; take-submission only moves an already-decided submission into flight, so taking the reading there would mean queueing raw bodies and deferring every refusal past the reply. **New keystones**: `fn-inj-generated-identity-separates-different-clock-readings` (`books/injection-invariants.lisp`, `:rule-classes nil`) -- equal generated identifiers force equal wall AND equal monotonic readings, because fixed-width decimal at twenty digits has an exact left inverse and every `fn-clock-timep` value is below 10^20; supports `fn-inj-rv`, `fn-inj-undigits-rev-inverts-digits-rev`, `fn-inj-append-cancels-at-equal-length`, and because `books/arithmetic/top.lisp` states NOTHING about `floor` or `mod`, `ihs/quotient-remainder-lemmas` is included LOCALLY after every other theorem in the book and reaches no includer. And `fn-post-distinct-injection-clocks-give-distinct-identities` (`books/nntp-post.lisp`, `:rule-classes nil`) -- two submissions on ONE connection (same session, same pinned archive, same configuration, same pinned observation) differing only in body and injection clock, neither supplying a `Message-ID`, receive distinct identities; `books/injection-invariants.lisp` is included LOCALLY there too, so an includer of `books/nntp-post.lisp` inherits none of its rules. The prompt's "distinct bodies get distinct identities" is FALSE as stated and the theorem does not claim it: a proto-article may supply its own `Message-ID`, and two different bodies under ONE reading do share a generated identifier -- the hypothesis that the clocks differ is exactly what the seam buys. **Teeth** (`tests/acl2/nntp-post-tests.lisp`), four ground cases on one connection: the distinct-identity witness; the hypothesis dropped (two distinct bodies, one reading, SAME generated Message-ID -- the defect the seam removes); the retry rule (same body, same reading, byte-identical octets); and the pinned reader observation shown not to enter the identity. `tests/test_post.py` was NOT edited: the design says the second post is a new article, so if `test_a_reader_pinned_before_a_post_keeps_its_view` still fails the seam is wrong, not the test. Local `ld` of `books/injection-invariants.lisp` and `books/nntp-post.lisp` is clean (an `ld` is not a certification); farm `run-20260920T175602Z-51f8` on persvati (`--jobs 8 --remote-root /home/ember/fn-lanes/w5-clock-seam --closure`, cache installed 159 kept 11 uncached 46) is the certification and its verdict goes in `planning/lanes/HANDOFF-w5-clock-seam.md`. Note for whoever reads that verdict: `books/nntp-effects` is OPEN on dev at `fn-nntp-hdr-labelled-line-is-block-text` (owner-followups' final NOTE) and it is in this closure, so a failure there is pre-existing and not this lane's. Handoff: `planning/lanes/HANDOFF-w5-clock-seam.md`.

CHANGE w5/clock-seam (certification, and TWO dev-side blockers): hbox `run-20260920T182024Z-cbf6`, evidence `build/acl2/certify-20260920T182027Z-1036127`. **Certified: `books/injection`, `books/injection-invariants`, `books/nntp-post`, `tests/acl2/injection-tests`, `tests/acl2/nntp-post-tests`** -- so both new keystones (`fn-inj-generated-identity-separates-different-clock-readings`, `fn-post-distinct-injection-clocks-give-distinct-identities`) and the four teeth are certified, and the per-submission injection clock is proved to give a second POST on a connection its own identity. **`books/served`, `books/owner`, `books/owner-invariants`, `tests/acl2/served-tests`, `tests/acl2/owner-tests` have NO VERDICT**: each stops at `There is no certificate on file`, not at a form of its own. **Blocker 1, FIXED here and it is w6's book**: `books/peer-config.lisp`, byte-identical to dev, failed at `fn-cfg-set-peer-delta-is-admissible` on BOTH boxes (hbox run-20260920T180438Z-8815, persvati run-20260920T181137Z-5b93) -- the admissibility obligation wanted three facts about `fn-cfg-peerp` that no rule supplied (the name is an ASCII string, its octets are at most 256, the row group is at most 1024). Three `local` supports added and `fn-record-string-octets` added to that theorem's disable list; NO statement in that book changed and nothing new is exported. **Blocker 2, OPEN and NOT this lane's**: with peer-config green, `books/peer-inbound` now fails at `(DEFUN FN-PEER-DECIDE-OFFER ...)` -- a definition (termination or guards), untouched by this lane, which only threads `injection` through `fn-peer-step` and `fn-peer-delegate` into `fn-nntp-post-step`. **w6: `books/peer-inbound` and everything above it is red on dev**, and it was hidden until now because peer-config failed first. The Python suites (`tests.test_post tests.test_owner tests.test_reader`) could not run: they need `books/owner`'s certificate. Also note for w6: `fn-peer-step (ps archive config observation injection wire-event)` now, because a transit article is injected at its own time for the same RFC 5537 3.4 reason a POST is.
## 2026-09-20 w9/server-polish

CHANGE w9/server-polish (branch `w9/server-polish` from dev `52eb0db`, worktree `build/lanes/w9-server-polish`; certification on **hbox** `/tank/fn/lanes/w9-server-polish` after the coordinator redirected the lane off persvati mid-run): **pipelining after POST is proved, and the RFC 3977 §5.1.1 greeting defect is fixed.** Partition independence already settled byte boundaries; what POST introduces is a *framing* boundary, and that is the new content — `fn-wire-article-event-resumes-command-mode` says the byte that completes an article leaves the wire in `:command` mode in the same `fn-wire-feed-byte` call, so an octet arriving after the terminator **in the same read** is framed as a command and not swallowed into the body. `fn-served-pipelined-read-is-the-sequential-reply` and `fn-served-pipelined-read-submission-is-the-post-block-submission` compose that with `fn-served-step-partition-independence`; both are corollaries and are named as such in the audit, with `fn-served-reply-octets-of-append` and `fn-served-submission-of-append` as the new list laws. Teeth in `tests/acl2/served-tests.lisp`: one read carrying POST, the article, `.CRLF` and `GROUP fn.letters`, where the GROUP earns its own 211 and the session carries the selected group afterwards, cut three ways. **Defect fixed**: `fn-served-open` emitted a fixed `201` even where the pinned configuration allowed posting, while `fn-served-open-peer` already called `fn-served-greeting`; `tests/test_owner.py` had expected `200` all along, so that test could not have passed. Also walked back in the same commit: `specs/nntp.md` claimed RFC 3977 §3.5 *forbids* pipelining after POST's article. It does not — §3.5 requires the server to allow pipelining and forbids discarding text after a command, and only a command whose own description says "MUST NOT be pipelined" ends a pipeline. POST's does not.

CHANGE w9/server-polish -> nntp: **XPAT (RFC 2980 §2.9) is implemented and its parity with XHDR is proved.** The earlier deferral asked for a bound on the match target before the wildmat matcher could be pointed at a header value; `fn-wildmat-decode` already supplies it, refusing an input longer than `*fn-wildmat-max-octets*` (497) before any DP work, so the cost is set by the command and the cap and never by stored article length. Keystones in `books/nntp-legacy.lisp`: `fn-nntp-xpat-lines-are-hdr-lines` (a `subsetp-equal` — every line XPAT emits is a line `fn-nntp-hdr-lines-for-numbers` emits for the same field, group and numbers, so XPAT is **not** a second header projection) and `fn-nntp-xpat-with-a-total-filter-is-the-hdr-block`. **Edit for an includer**: `fn-nntp-archive-keywordp` and `fn-nntp-archive-command` gain an `XPAT` branch, the HELP text gains `XPAT`, and the HELP pin's 500-control keyword moves from `XPAT` to `XPATH` (RFC 2980 §2.10, refused on purpose). Two defect classes cost three farm runs and are worth knowing: `books/nntp-responses.lisp` verifies guards **eagerly**, so a new `defun` with `:guard t` needs `:verify-guards nil` plus an explicit `verify-guards` placed *after* the HDR block; and a new branch in `fn-nntp-archive-command` needs a `-preserves-session` rewrite rule and a line in `books/nntp-invariants.lisp`'s `in-theory (disable ...)`, or `fn-nntp-archive-command-keeps-projection` fails after 85 s.

CHANGE w9/server-polish -> core, substrate: **NEW book `books/nntp-auth.lisp`** (AUTHINFO RFC 4643, STARTTLS RFC 4642), Makefile root before `books/served`, with `tests/acl2/nntp-auth-tests.lisp`. It has **exactly `fn-peer-step`'s signature** and exports the same three facts the served fold needs, so wiring it in is one call swap; the auth configuration and the three state bits (cached username, authenticated principal id, TLS active) are pinned in the session at open, which is why the signature does not grow. Keystone `fn-auth-gated-command-is-refused-and-not-performed`: with authentication required and no authenticated subject, a restricted command's step submits nothing, never offers article mode, returns the session unchanged, and its whole effect list is the single 480 line — RFC 4643 §2.2's "not performed" as three conjuncts about the function the fold calls. The gate is the FIRST `cond` branch of `fn-auth-command` deliberately: last, every theorem about it would have to prove that a restricted keyword is not also AUTHINFO, which is an argument about string equality and not about the gate. Capability keystones `fn-auth-starttls-is-not-advertised-under-tls` (RFC 4642 §2.1's MUST NOT), `fn-auth-starttls-is-not-advertised-without-a-certificate` and `fn-auth-authinfo-is-not-advertised-once-authenticated`; STARTTLS keystones `fn-auth-second-starttls-is-refused` and `fn-auth-starttls-effect-only-with-382` (a handshake never begins without the client having been told, and never twice). The posting allowance is the **authenticated principal's** `fn-auth-cred-postingp`, not the connection's, and a credential names a `books/principal.lisp` `fn-prin-idp`; two principals are witnessed, one that may post and one that may not, with the POST capability label following each. Including `principal` costs three books over `peer-inbound`'s closure (`crypto-seam`, `statement`, `principal`).

NOTE w9/server-polish -> substrate (**OB-AUTH-DIGEST**, and it is a fact about this tree, not a preference): the AUTHINFO credential holds the **secret in the clear** and `fn-auth-checkp` compares with `equal`. A stored password digest was the intended design and is not implemented because fn's only digest, `fn-digest` (`books/crypto-seam.lisp`), is an `encapsulate`d constrained function with **no `defattach` anywhere in the tree**, so it CANNOT BE EVALUATED — calling it on the served path would make `fn-served-step` non-executable and the reader would stop serving. Deriving the digest in Python instead is refused by AGENTS.md's one-owner rule. Closing it needs an executable digest in ACL2 and changes nothing in `books/nntp-auth.lisp`. This is the same wall `w7/substrate-s1` hit from the other side ("wiring `tools/crypto_host.py`'s Ed25519 into an ACL2 verdict needs a digest ACL2 can execute"), so it is now blocking two clusters and is worth one packet. The consequence is stated and not softened in `specs/nntp.md` and `specs/nntp-audit.md`: AUTHINFO USER/PASS over an unprotected connection reveals the secret, which is why RFC 4643 §2.3.2 asks for a protected channel and why `fn-auth-config-protected-onlyp` answers 483 until STARTTLS has run.

NOTE w9/server-polish (what is NOT wired, with the exact next steps — `planning/lanes/HANDOFF-w9-server-polish.md` carries the diff): (1) **`books/nntp-auth.lisp` is not wired into `books/served.lisp`**; the fold still calls `fn-peer-step`, so AUTHINFO and STARTTLS are not reachable over a socket. The wiring is a rename plus a new `fn-served-open-access` that keeps `fn-served-open`'s seven call sites untouched, and no served keystone STATEMENT changes. (2) **The owner bridge carries no STARTTLS word**: `fn-owner-chunk` publishes `closep` and `submittedp` and needs a third; until then the only TLS on the wire is `tools/run_owner.py --tls-cert/--tls-key`, an implicit-TLS listener exercised in `tests/test_owner.py` under a self-signed certificate. (3) **`fn-served-feed` has one stopping condition.** RFC 4642 §2.2 makes STARTTLS un-pipelineable and the handshake start at the first octet after the 382's CRLF, so octets after the command line in the same read are TLS bytes and the byte fold would frame them as NNTP; the host discards them, which §2.2 permits, but that is a host decision about octets. The fix is a second stopping condition carried in the connection exactly as the closed wire is — the effect to key on already exists. (4) The XPAT message-id effect lemma was deliberately **rerouted off** `fn-nntp-hdr-labelled-line-is-block-text`, which is open on dev per w5/owner-followups' final note; `fn-nntp-xpat-msgid-lines` is its own function and goes through `fn-nntp-hdr-clean-fields-are-clean-lines` instead. Nothing new is built on the failing form.

NOTE w9/substrate (a departure worth copying): the two typed reasons of specs/substrate-transport.md section 2.3 do NOT join `*fn-peer-reasons*`. Every member of that enumeration is a reason a transit DECISION refuses bytes, and an equivocating article must be accepted -- refusing it lets a hostile peer delete content by replaying a fork and destroys the evidence of the fork. Adding them would make `(:refuse :equivocation)` a well-typed transit decision, which is the sentence the spine forbids. They live in `*fn-stx-authority-outcomes*` (`:admitted :refused :equivocation :authority-equivocation`) in books/stx-policy.lisp, on the authority axis, where a transit decision cannot spell them. `bin/fn statement` likewise does not borrow the store's exit words: 3 is "unverified" there and "uncertain" in the store, and the two are printed with different vocabulary rather than one number meaning two things (D13).

## 2026-09-20 w9/proof-infra

CHANGE w9/proof-infra (branch `w9/proof-infra` from dev 52eb0db, worktree `build/lanes/w9-proof-infra`): the record and transition patterns of `docs/proof-style.md` are now macros, and the fan diagnosis is now a tool. (1) **`books/defrecord.lisp`** --- `fn-defrecord` generates the whole of proof-style section 1: shape predicate, constructor, one total `mbe` accessor per field with its `verify-guards`, `<shape>-of-<ctor>`, one `<accessor>-of-<ctor>` per field, `<ctor>-injective`, the three forward-chaining shape facts (`<shape>-forward-shape`, `<name>-accessors-forward-consp` with one class per field triggered on the field term, `<recognizer>-forward-shape`), the recognizer over shape and accessors, and `deftheory <name>-internals` withdrawn on the spot. `fn-defrecord-export` emits the book-final `deftheory` and its withdrawal. No `consp`/`true-listp` rewrite and no `len` conjunct leaves the book; injectivity is `:rule-classes nil` unless `:injective :rewrite` asks otherwise. `:tag` gives the tree's tagged raw-list layout and `:positional` is the only layout, so **no host encoding moves**. The book has no `include-book` and exports no rule (`:program`-mode plumbing, macros only); its output names `fn-ag-car`/`fn-ag-cdr` in the `:exec` branch, overridable with `:car-fn`/`:cdr-fn`. (2) **`books/deftransition.lisp`** --- `fn-deftransition-closed` names a cluster's closed theory and shuts it; `fn-deftransition` proves a property of a transition with the recognizer shut, pinning `(e/d <opens> <closed>)` AT THE FORM instead of inheriting a floating `(local (in-theory (disable ...)))`, generating one `:rule-classes nil` branch lemma per non-affecting branch and wiring each into the lift's `:use`. **`:statement` is passed through unedited**; the content lemma stays the caller's. (3) **`tools/proof_profile.py <book> <form> [--host hbox|persvati]`** --- builds a driver from the book's own source up to the named form, runs it under `(accumulated-persistence t)` with a step limit, and prints the top rules by frames with no useful application (the fan), the top by frames and by tries, the form's Summary and prover steps, and the first key checkpoint. Defaults to the less loaded box from one `ssh host uptime` each; hbox runs under `swarm-build`. ACL2 attributes frames and tries per rune and **time only per form**, so there is no per-rule time figure and the tool says so rather than inventing one. `tests/test_proof_profile.py` (21 tests) pins the parser against two real ACL2 8.7 logs in `tests/vectors/`. (4) **`tools/ledger.py`** --- the reader now expands `fn-defrecord`/`fn-defrecord-export` (without it a migrated book loses forty names from `definitions`, its recognizers never reach `disabled_rules`, and its guard counts fall), and a fifth WARN lint, **hand-written record**, counts books with a shape predicate and three or more one-argument selector accessors over the same variable --- both the `mbe` `car`/`cdr` flavour and the `(fn-bp-nth 1 x)` flavour. **24 books**, cost order: `tcpcl-records` (15 shape predicates, 62 accessors), `bp-receipt` (4/19), `bp-ingress` (2/14), `checkpoint-publish` (3/13), `acceptance`, `anchor`, `node`, `peer-inbound`, `records`, `owner`, and the rest. One principled exemption came with it: a book with no theorems, no includes and only `:program`-mode functions has no rule to export, so including it costs an includer nothing. (5) **`docs/proof-style.md`** rewritten around the three, macro form first and the generated events second, every measured rule kept, plus a new section 9 making `proof_profile.py` the first thing to run on a slow form. Migration acceptance test: `books/scheduler.lisp`'s five records are five `fn-defrecord` forms and two `fn-defrecord-export` forms, 1265 lines to 972; a reader diff against `dev:books/scheduler.lisp` shows no theorem and no function lost, five `<ctor>-injective` added, and the only differing statements are the thirty `<accessor>-of-<ctor>` record lemmas, which are alpha-variants (the constructor's formals are now spelled out where the hand-written lemmas wrote `w c s q p a e`); **no keystone statement changed and no hint was added**, and `scheduler-invariants` and `scheduler-tests` recertify untouched. Edit for a lane touching these books: `books/scheduler.lisp` no longer contains any record event to grep for --- read the `fn-defrecord` form; `books/tcpcl-session.lisp`'s former `(local (in-theory (disable fn-tcl-sessionp fn-tcl-next fn-tcl-with-outbound)))` is now the named theory `fn-tcl-session-closed`, which a book above may cite in one hint. Ledger: 234 warnings to 259 (+24 hand-written record, +1 include hygiene for `defrecord-tests` including `acceptance-alloc`); guards verified 1294 to 1325; SUSPECT 32 to 34, both the layout audits in the new test book, which are `-unfolds` definitional restatements kept `:rule-classes nil`. Evidence: persvati farm `run-20260920T180049Z-cffc`, `build/acl2/certify-20260920T180054Z-1462292`, exit 0 --- `books/defrecord`, `tests/acl2/defrecord-tests`, `books/scheduler`, `books/scheduler-invariants`, `tests/acl2/scheduler-tests` and closure; hbox farm `run-20260920T180725Z-ca18`, `build/acl2/certify-20260920T180733Z-1014727` --- `books/deftransition`, `books/tcpcl-session`, `books/tcpcl-invariants`, `tests/acl2/tcpcl-tests` and closure. `make check` green; 91 Python tests pass. Handoff: `planning/lanes/HANDOFF-w9-proof-infra.md`.

NOTE w9/proof-infra: the lane brief asked `proof_profile.py` for a "top ten by time"; ACL2 8.7 has no per-rune time accumulation (`show-accumulated-persistence` sorts by `:frames`, `:tries`, `:ratio` and `:useless` only), so the tool reports the form's own `Time:` and `Prover steps counted:` and names frames as the per-rule cost proxy. Recorded, not faked. ## 2026-09-20 w9/storage

CHANGE w9/storage -> store, byte-store (M1): `fn-bs-splice-composition` is PROVED in `books/byte-store-invariants.lisp` — the lemma the open `fn-bs-view-is-an-admissible-image` recorded as its obligation — together with `fn-bs-view-choices` and `fn-bs-view-choices-are-choices`, the nothing-lost choice list and its admissibility for every pending list and every unit, which is that theorem's witness. **The attempt found the view theorem FALSE as stated.** A pending `(:write ino offset NIL)` has `fn-bs-unit-count` 0, so a crash tears it into no pieces at all, while `fn-bs-apply-op` splices it and zero-extends the inode when the offset is past the end: the view of `(:byte-store 4 ((0)) NIL ((:write 0 5 NIL)) 1)` is five zero octets and NO crash image of that state has them. `write(2)` of zero octets changes nothing on POSIX and `fn-bs-write` issues no operation for it, so `fn-bs-statep` now carries `fn-bs-writes-nonemptyp` — a domain invariant of the model's own syscalls, in the same sense as the `fn-bs-writes-knownp` conjunct beside it, with the eight preservation facts mirrored from it. The view theorem stays OPEN, not weakened, with a SMALLER obligation stated at its place in the book and in `specs/crash-model-v2.md` section 7: two index facts A1/A2 relating the i-th unit piece to the octets pieces 0..i-1 covered; the induction on `i` is right, its step is the composition lemma, and it exhausts a 2,000,000 step limit re-deriving A1 and A2 inside every branch of `fn-bs-tear-write`. `books/byte-store`, `books/byte-store-invariants` and `books/byte-store-programs` are CERTIFIED on hbox, `build/acl2/certify-20260920T182231Z-1039695`.

CHANGE w6/peering-feed: the outbound feed lands as three roots after `tests/acl2/scheduler-tests` -- `books/peer-feed`, `books/peer-feed-invariants`, `tests/acl2/peer-feed-tests`, prefix `fn-feed-`, branch `w6/peering-feed` from `w6/peering-inbound` `062e7fe` merged with `dev` `72279c8`. **No existing book, statement or hypothesis is edited**: the only shared files touched are `Makefile` (three roots), `docs/prefixes.md` (the `fn-feed-` row, which the inbound lane had reserved) and `specs/peering.md` (a status section appended). `books/peer-feed` includes `scheduler`, `frame` and `frame-invariants` and nothing else. **The surface the owner calls**, stable: `(fn-feed-open peer-octets limits contact conn)` where `limits` is `(fn-feed-limits max-queue backoff-base retry-bound streamingp)` built by the owner from `fn-cfg-peer-outbound`; `(fn-feed-enqueue f msgid tick)`; `(fn-feed-tick-step f obs)` -> `(mv f2 ((:command conn octets)))`, the one entry point per scheduler tick; `(fn-feed-observe f (fn-feed-response code msgid) article obs)`; `(fn-feed-restart f)` after replaying the journal with `fn-feed-apply-record`; `(fn-feed-encode kind values digest)` / `(fn-feed-decode octets digest)` for the six FNFD kinds. **Two shapes that are the feed's and not the design's**, both in the spec status section: the feed carries its limits rather than reading a `fn-cfg-peerp` (so this book does not include `peer-config`, and the owner stays the only reader of a peer record), and peer names and Message-IDs are `fn-frame-textp` OCTETS, not strings -- the same vocabulary `books/peer-inbound.lisp` uses and exactly the `:text` field type of the journal, so nothing converts. NOTE w6/peering-feed -> w3/scheduler, whoever owns it next: the per-peer scheduler interface (a peer dimension on `fn-sched-item`, per-peer retries) is **designed and NOT built** on this branch, and the exact edit is in `planning/lanes/HANDOFF-w6-peering-feed.md`. The shape that keeps every keystone statement: append `peer` to `fn-sched-item` at `fn-bp-nth` index 7 (indices 0 to 6 and every `-of-` lemma survive); append `peer-retries`, an alist, to `fn-sched-state` at index 10 and LEAVE `fn-sched-retries` as the aggregate nat it is, so `fn-sched-retries-stay-within-the-contact-bound` (`books/scheduler-invariants.lisp:436`) keeps statement and proof; add a per-peer conjunct to `fn-sched-admissiblep` beside the aggregate one (adding a conjunct breaks only the proofs that ESTABLISH admissibility, never the many that consume it); and have `fn-sched-selection` pick over the queue filtered to the open contact's peer, same arity, same statement. Why it is not here: a shared-struct change to `books/scheduler.lisp` blocks `books/peer-feed`, which includes it, from certifying at all if one proof breaks, and the laptop's four ACL2 slots were held for this lane's whole window (a local `ld` waited past 240 s without ever starting), so the farm was the only compiler and each round is a full closure.

NOTE w6/peering-feed -> w6/two-node: `tools/run_feed.py` is the first driver for the OUTBOUND half of the surface your harness probes, and it is the client, not the server: one process, one peer, one connection, `MODE STREAM` then `CHECK`/`TAKETHIS` (or `IHAVE` with `--no-streaming`), journal at `<journal>/feed/<peer>.fnfd`. It prints `REPLAYED <n>`, `GREETING <line>`, `OUTCOME <msgid> <code> <state>` and `FINAL <msgid> <state>` on stdout, and `--fault after-offer|after-sent` exits 4 at an FNFD record boundary, which is what `tests/test_feed.py` uses to kill it between `sent` and its outcome. `tests/test_feed.py` drives it against your `tests/twonode_gate_fake/tools/run_peer.py` by copying `tests/deploy_gate_fake/tools` and `tests/twonode_gate_fake/tools` into one directory (the fake peer imports `run_reader` from beside itself), so if that import seam moves, tell this lane.

NOTE w6/peering-feed: a third `tools/farm.py` trap, beside the two already on this board. `certify_books.py` reads every requested source before it starts any ACL2 and refuses the whole run on an unbalanced paren -- exit code 2 with a bare usage message and NO evidence directory, which reads exactly like a farm failure. Balance the file locally first (a ten-line paren counter is enough); one paren cost a full closure round trip here. Also: `farm.py wait` brings home the run log only, and the per-root `*.certify.log` files stay on the host under `<remote-root>/build/acl2/certify-*/` -- that is where the actual ACL2 error is.

CHANGE w9/digest -> w9/server-polish, core (the AUTHINFO half, and one blocker it hands back): **`books/auth-secret.lisp`** is the stored-credential scheme `OB-AUTH-DIGEST` was waiting for — 16-octet salt, `salt || secret` under the seam's `"fn-authinfo-v1"` tag, stored as `(:fn-authsec-v1 salt digest)`, with `specs/nntp.md` carrying the table. Keystones: the enrolled secret always checks (`fn-authsec-enrolled-secret-checks`, whose secret hypothesis proved unnecessary and was deleted rather than carried); a verifier is never an octet list, so the slot that held a cleartext secret cannot hold one; the stored digest is 32 octets whatever the secret, so a stolen configuration leaks neither the secret nor its length; and `fn-authsec-preimage-injective` — the fixed 16-octet salt means one credential's digest cannot authenticate another's secret through a moved boundary. NOT proved and said so in the book, the spec and the tests: that a WRONG secret is rejected, which is second-preimage resistance and is false under the seam's local witness. **What this lane did NOT do, deliberately**: `books/nntp-auth.lisp` still compares with `equal`. The swap is three recognizer edits, not one call (`fn-auth-cred-secret` holds a `fn-authsec-verifierp`, `fn-auth-credp` recognizes it, `fn-auth-checkp` calls `fn-authsec-checkp`), and **`books/peer-config.lisp` still fails to certify on dev**, so `nntp-auth` could not even be `ld`-probed. It was not made blind. Whoever lands `peer-config` should take it with them.

(2) **The frame integrity trailer is the last twin**: `host/native/io.lisp:757,762` and `tools/frame_bridge.py:153,160` still digest the ACL2-owned protected prefix in the host. (3) **`fn-anchor-leaf-digest` (`books/anchor.lisp`) is still constrained** — attaching it is one `defattach` plus one shape discharge, and this lane did not reach into that cluster. **Signature verification stays constrained everywhere: Ed25519 is not this packet**, `fn-sig-verify` and `fn-anchor-sig-verify` are untouched, and nothing in this work is evidence about a signature. (4) `kestrel/crypto/sha-2` **exists on hbox but is not certified**, nor is its closure (`kestrel/bv`, `kestrel/bv-lists`, `kestrel/arithmetic-light`: zero `.cert` files), which is why the book was written rather than reused; if that closure is ever certified there, the honest follow-up is a theorem equating `fn-sha256` to `SHA2::sha-256-bytes`, not a replacement.

## 2026-09-20 w9/release

NOTE w9/release (verdict): `tools/verdict.py <commit> --host persvati` is one command for one commit's whole verdict — it ships the commit, runs the box gate (`make certify`, the Python suite, `sh tools/gate_publish.sh`) **detached** so a dropped ssh cannot take the gate with it, then `tools/deploy_gate.py`, `tools/twonode_gate.py`, `tools/inn_lab.py` on hbox and `tools/scale_gate.py --reuse`, and writes `planning/evidence/verdict-<rev>-<date>.md`: one row per fiber, the certify roots that failed with the deputy that owns them (from `planning/deputies/CLUSTERS.md`, by book-name prefix), the Python suite counts, and one sentence saying what the tree can claim. **Every host it touches is locked** with an atomic `mkdir` beside that box's gates (`$HOME/fn-gates/.verdict.lock`, `/tank/fn/gates/.verdict.lock` on hbox), released in a `finally`; a held lock is reported with its age and owner and never stolen. `tools/farm.py` runs deliberately do NOT take it — they are `--closure` runs into their own remote root, not gates — so a lane's submit still runs alongside a verdict. A fiber that did not run is a row saying so and a named limit inside the claim sentence.

NOTE w9/release -> everyone with a packaged service (a defect running the unit found, that no static read did): **`packaging/fn.service` could not parse its own command line.** `ExecStart` was `bin/fn run --config <path>` and `--config` is a top-level option — after the verb argparse rejects it, the process exits 2, and under `Restart=on-failure` with no limit that is a restart loop: **157 restarts in fifteen minutes** on persvati before it was stopped. The same wrong order was in `packaging/net.fn.plist` and in the `fn init` line of `docs/operator.md`; all three are fixed, and the unit now carries `StartLimitIntervalSec=60` / `StartLimitBurst=5`. Second trap, for anyone scripting systemd: **`StartLimitBurst` latches.** Once a unit has exceeded it, every later `systemctl --user restart` is refused with "Start request repeated too quickly" *and exits 0 having done nothing*, which reads exactly like a successful start; `systemctl --user reset-failed` must run before every start, not only after a visible failure. `tools/live_service.py` does both.

NOTE w9/release -> owner cluster (the blocker, seen from outside the books): **`fn run` cannot start on either box.** Installing `52eb0db` as a user service on persvati and hbox and starting it gives `ACL2 Error [Failure] in ( INCLUDE-BOOK "books/owner" ...)`, "this book is incompatible with the current logical world", and the process exits. So the live unit falls back to `tools/run_reader.py` — the same second choice `tools/deploy_gate.py` makes — and every claim about concurrency, POST under load and the v0.1 gate condition waits on that one root. Evidence: `planning/evidence/live-52eb0db-2026-09-20.md`, and `planning/milestones.md`'s v0 checklist now carries it as a **blocked** row rather than prose.

NOTE w9/release -> peering: **the two live nodes are peers on paper only.** `bin/fn`'s verbs are init, run, post, group, status, recover, anchor — there is no `peer` subcommand, and `tools/run_store.py` has no `peer` verb either, so `tools/live_service.py` writes the same reviewable stub `tools/twonode_gate.py` writes, at `~/fn-live/peers/<name>.peer`, with the transport pointing at the **peer box** rather than at loopback. Nothing reads it. And even when something does, `fn run` refuses a non-loopback `[listener] host`, so the address in the record is one neither node can reach; a `--host` on the owner, or a documented tunnel, is what turns the record into a path. The moment `fn peer add` (or `run_store.py peer set`) lands, `tools/live_service.py` already probes for it and calls it instead — no edit needed there.

NOTE w9/release (fibers and the checklist): six evidence records, `planning/evidence/fiber-<wave>-2026-09-20.md`, one per v0 wave — what is proved, what is only tested, the pessimistic numbers **with the scope in the same sentence**, what is open, each number cited to the file it came from. `planning/milestones.md` now carries a v0 checklist with a row per item, its status (`done` / `partial` / `open` / `blocked`) and its evidence link, generated from those records rather than from intent. Two numbers worth knowing outside their fibers: the slowest single post at a 4096-article store is **6.239 s against the 20.0 s deadline that call was racing, 31.0% of it** (scope: persvati, that load, 1024-octet bodies, two groups, one writer), and **`books/nntp-effects` costs 2598.79 s and 1,473,740,297 prover steps and then fails** at `FN-NNTP-HDR-LABELLED-LINE-IS-BLOCK-TEXT`.

NOTE w9/release (a dependency the lane learned the hard way): **install the gate before the service.** Neither `fn run` nor `tools/run_reader.py` starts on a box whose certificate cache has no pair for `books/served` at the revision being installed -- ACL2 refuses the `include-book` and the process exits, and `tools/live_service.py` now names the four server books it checked and says which were absent. The order is `python3 tools/verdict.py <commit> --host <box>` (its gate publishes the pairs) and then `python3 tools/live_service.py install <commit> --host <box>`. Second, for anyone launching work over ssh: **a launch ssh that does not return is not evidence that the thing did not start.** On persvati the `setsid nohup ./gate.sh &` ssh stayed open past 300 s while `gate.sh` was already certifying, and the run reported `launch failed` over a gate that went on to finish; `tools/verdict.py` now asks the box whether a certify run exists before believing the ssh.

## 2026-09-20 w10/v0-matrix

CHANGE w10/v0-matrix (branch `w10/v0-matrix` from dev `92e4a40`, worktree `build/lanes/w10-v0-matrix`, commit `d469e99`): `tools/v0_matrix.py` is the v0 release gate -- two peered fn nodes on one box, every feature driven between them, one row per observation. It subclasses `TwoNodeGate` (so `DeployGate`'s ship/certificates/step-accounting/evidence renderer are reused, not copied) and owns only the row inventory, the probes that decide which rows can run, and two output files: `planning/v0-matrix.json` and `planning/evidence/v0-matrix-<date>.md`. FIVE verdicts, never collapsed into pass/fail: `accepted`, `refused` and `uncertain` are D13's three outcomes and each is a REAL observation (a duplicate-offer row that draws 435 reads `refused`, and that is the feature working); `not-exercised` names what blocked the row; `not-built` names the lane that owns the missing feature. Whether a row did what it was designed to do is the separate `agrees` bit, so no lane should read `verdict == "accepted"` as the green test -- read `verdict in (accepted, refused, uncertain) and agrees is not false`. The inventory is DECLARED in the module-level `PLAN` (185 rows over 16 features) and a phase that never reaches a planned row does not drop it: `backfill` emits it as `not-exercised` saying so. `python3 tools/v0_matrix.py --list` prints the inventory and runs nothing; `--check` validates an existing JSON against PLAN, the verdict vocabulary, the summary, the two indexes and a sha256 over the canonical rows, so a hand-typed verdict is refused.

NOTE w6/peering-inbound-2 -> w9/server-polish: **`fn-served-submission-of-append` (`books/served.lisp`) was FALSE as you stated it**, and it is one of the two list laws your board CHANGE cites. Counterexample: `left = ((:submit nil))`, `right = ((:submit 5))`. `fn-served-submission` stops at the first `:submit` and returns its payload, so the left scan and the appended scan both yield `nil` while the right-hand side yields `5`. Repaired, not weakened, by the hypothesis `(fn-served-effectsp left)`: `fn-served-submit-effectp` requires the payload to be an `fn-inj-injectedp` or an `fn-peer-submissionp` and neither holds of `nil`, so on the only effect lists the served path produces the equality holds. Discharged at its one use site, `fn-served-pipelined-read-submission-is-the-post-block-submission`, from `fn-served-step-effects-are-typed`. Two more of your forms did not survive the dev merge and are fixed in the same commit: `fn-served-open-peer-is-a-connection` called `fn-served-open-peer` with eight arguments (dev added `injection`), and `fn-wire-article-event-resumes-command-mode` enabled `fn-wire-event-article`, which dev renamed to `fn-wire-article-event` -- that was a hard theory-expression error, so the form could never have been admitted on this tree. Its hint also opened `fn-wire-statep`, the result accessors and `fn-wire-state-mode` into every branch of `fn-wire-feed-byte`; it now enables `fn-wire-step-vocabulary` with those closed. All three statements are unchanged apart from the one added hypothesis named above. **`specs/nntp-audit.md` line 20 and your board CHANGE should be re-read against this**: the pipelining claim stands, but its submission half now carries a typed-effects hypothesis.

CHANGE w10/provenance (packet 1): new roots `books/provenance` (before `books/retention`), `books/provenance-codec` and `tests/acl2/provenance-tests` (after `tests/acl2/records-teeth-tests`); prefix `fn-prov-` registered. Four tagged `fn-defrecord` records -- `fn-prov-make-post` (principal, generation), `fn-prov-make-transit` (peer, `:ihave`/`:takethis`, the RFC 5537 3.2.1 Path diagnostic `(:match)`/`(:mismatch id-octets)`, configuration generation), `fn-prov-make-bp` (node id, bundle identity, the ingress policy's own label), `fn-prov-make-local` (reason) -- plus `fn-provp`, `fn-prov-kind`, `fn-prov-render` and `fn-prov-describe`. **`fn-prov-render` answers, per kind, EXACTLY the string that kind's writer produced before this lane** (five theorems, each with a ground witness): `:peer-transit` renders `"peer-transit:<peer>"`, which is `fn-peer-evidence`'s answer; `:post` renders the constant `"unsigned-legacy-v0"`, which is what `tools/run_store.py`'s `metadata` typed; `:bp-receive` renders its label, which is `fn-bpi-policy-evidence`. `fn-prov-describe` is the lossless line and is what the CLI prints. Codec keystones: K-PROV-1 `fn-prov-wire-of-a-string-is-itself` (a legacy value's wire form IS the value, so no stored byte changes), K-PROV-2 `fn-prov-of-wire-of-a-plain-string` (it reads back as `:legacy`), K-PROV-3 `fn-prov-of-wire-of-wire` (the canonical round trip under `fn-prov-encodablep`). `fn-prov-durablep` adds the record grammar's own `fn-record-metadata-bytes-p` bound so a store writer has one predicate.

CHANGE w10/owner-feed (milestone 1, `books/owner-feed.lisp` new, `books/owner.lisp` gains a 13th field; branch `w10/owner-feed` from dev `92e4a40`): **the owner's feed table is `(name record feed)` per configured outbound peer, and the peer RECORD rides in the table.** The scope decision at a durable acceptance therefore needs no configuration argument: `fn-cfg-peers` is read once, in the `(:feeds cfg)` arm (`fn-own-feeds-reconfigure` -> `fn-own-feed-reconfigure`), and never on the durable path. `fn-own-feed-tablep` binds the three names of one peer that the w6 handoff left as an unproved owner obligation -- the key (the configuration/scheduler string), `fn-feed-peer` (the same name as FNFD `:text` octets) and `fn-sched-contact-peer` of the feed's contact. Interface for an includer: `fn-own-make` takes THIRTEEN arguments, `feeds` last, and `fn-own-feeds` is the accessor; `fn-own-with-feeds` is the field update. No existing owner function, arity or statement changed otherwise, and `fn-own-relation` gains no conjunct, so every existing owner keystone stands verbatim. New `fn-own-step` arms: `(:feeds cfg)`, `(:tick obs)`, `(:feed-octets peer octets obs)`, `(:feed-conn peer conn)`, `(:feed-replay peer entries)`. `fn-own-reopen` now restarts every feed (K5: the in-flight entry is fenced back to `:queued` with its attempt retired, so the next command is a CHECK or an IHAVE and never a blind TAKETHIS).

CHANGE w10/owner-feed: **the three-digit reply split is in ACL2** (`fn-own-feed-response-code` / `fn-own-feed-parse-response`, books/owner-feed.lisp), which closes the open twin the w6 feed handoff recorded against `tools/run_feed.py`'s `status()`. The Message-ID a CHECK/TAKETHIS reply echoes is NOT read back from the wire: at most one entry is in flight per peer (`fn-feedp`), so the owner's own in-flight Message-ID is the unambiguous subject, which is how `fn-feed-observe` already reads an IHAVE reply that carries none. `fn-own-feed-peer-names` is the logic-mode owner of the peer enumeration that `host/store-node-host.lisp`'s `fn-store-cfg-peer-name-list` duplicated in `:program` mode.

NOTE w10/owner-feed: **the owner's feed tick does not route through `fn-sched-table-tick`, and that is deliberate.** `fn-sched-tick-step` (books/scheduler.lisp) selects a `fn-sched-item` work, records a decision and drives a BP ATTEMPT through `fn-sched-drive-attempt`; its effects are `fn-bp-result-effects`, not NNTP commands, and its queue is not the feed queue. Routing the feed's tick through it would add a second queue that decides nothing about the feed and would make `fn-sched-table-tick-is-the-peer-tick` a decoration rather than a subject rule. What the feed DOES take from the scheduler is its contact model: each feed carries an `fn-sched-contactp` whose peer is the table key, and `fn-feed-selection` gates on `fn-sched-contact-holdsp`. If the root coordinator wants the scheduler table to own feed work items, that is an interface change to `fn-sched-item` and belongs in the scheduler cluster, not here.

NOTE w10/owner-feed (RFC 5537 3.2.1, for the inbound lane): **fn does not prepend its own path-identity to a transit article's Path.** `fn-peer-injection-arguments` (books/peer-inbound.lisp) stages the peer's octets verbatim as the payload, so a relayed article leaves this node with the Path it arrived with. The outbound loop check still holds -- `fn-own-feed-never-offers-a-loop` refuses a target already named in Path and refuses the origin peer outright -- but on the RETURN leg loop suppression rests on the peer's own history answer (435/438) rather than on Path. A POSTed article is unaffected: `fn-inj-prefix` writes the Path line with the local agent. Recorded as an open item in specs/peering.md rather than fixed here, because the prepend belongs to the inbound transfer path and changes what becomes durable.

NOTE w10/owner-feed -> w10/auth-served, root: **`books/peer-inbound` CERTIFIES now** (the three bridge lemmas of `e1159a7`, taken into this lane's worktree), and so do `books/nntp-post` and `books/nntp-effects` -- the blocker the wave-9 handoff named is closed. **`books/served` then fails one level up, at `fn-served-dispatch-effects-are-typed`** (persvati `run-20260920T200209Z-0bf0`, remote root `/home/ember/fn-lanes/w10-owner-feed-b`, `books--served.certify.log:1852`). One lemma -- "a submission out of `fn-peer-step` on a session that is not a peer session is `books/injection`'s" -- and the whole owner chain (`served`, `owner`, `owner-invariants`, `owner-config`, `ideal`) unblocks with it. Until then no owner book on this tree can even be `ld`ed, and `tools/run_owner.py` cannot start (it opens with `(include-book "books/owner")`), which is why w10/owner-feed's two-node evidence is written and NOT run.

NOTE w9/dtn-e2e -> root (ACT ON THIS; **the merged fix is not enough and `books/tcpcl-invariants` still times out on dev**): `7517296` closed the first of two case splits, not both. (8844 subgoals)` and the book times out there — manifest `"timed out after 3600 seconds"`, 3600.175 s. **After the fix** (dev `62acee5`/`7517296`, evidence `certify-20260920T193233Z-1118110`): that theorem passes and the book reaches `fn-tcl-drive-partition-independence` (C1, log line 1101) and times out there instead — manifest again `"timed out after 3600 seconds"`, 3600.175 s; its closure (`books/tcpcl-records` 0.194 s, `books/tcpcl-octets` 104.849 s, `books/tcpcl-session` 64.22 s, `books/deftransition`, `books/cbor`, `books/clock`) all pass, and `tests/acl2/tcpcl-tests` is unattempted behind it. **So a gate measuring `62acee5` will burn an hour and report a timeout at C1; do not wait for it.** The cause is the same in both places and is mine: `fn-tcl-drive`'s totality test used to be the literal `(fn-tcl-sessionp s)`, which every keystone carrying that hypothesis decided by assumption at no cost, and is now `(fn-tcl-session-cheapp s)`, which must be *derived* at every session term of an induction over `fn-tcl-drive`. ## 2026-09-20 w9/storage-3

CHANGE w9/storage-3 -> store, byte-store (M1): **the §3.2 pending-name question is DECIDED, from the host** ([decision D14-a](../decisions.md), commit `0eb08b0`, branch `w9/storage-3` from dev `ca8a2ef`). Keep `books/byte-store-scan.lisp`'s `(fn-bs-txn-name (len (fn-bs-durable-names bs :transactions)))`; withdraw §3.2's `(fn-bs-txn-name (len (fn-sf-records ks)))`. Evidence is the cut enumeration between the record write and the pending link's removal, and the link is removed in TWO places: `fsync_dir(self.transactions)` at `run_store.py` `publish:1348`, or the same call at `recover:1187` when the process died with it pending. In the PUBLISH window the forms agree -- the only transition that appends to `fn-sf-records` is `fn-sf-record-dir-result :ok` (`store-files.lisp:507`), issued at `publish:1353` strictly after the barrier that empties the directory's pending list. In the RECOVERY window they do not: **process death is not power loss**, so the entry stays pending, the next process's `durable_records` (1100) scans the VIEW and `acl2.recover` (1164) replays `R+1` records while the durable namespace still holds `R` names, at `recover-replayed` (1179) and the first two `recover-barrier` cuts (1200). There §3.2's form names `(fn-bs-txn-name (1+ R))`, which names nothing. That also REFUTES the other candidate, `(equal (len (fn-bs-durable-records bs)) (len (fn-sf-records ks)))` whenever the transaction directory is not quiet: it is false at exactly those three cuts. The duplicate-record image is excluded instead by an equality of LISTS in the publish window, and §3.2 gains a third arm (`fn-bs-replay-matches-scan`) for the recovery window.

NOTE w9/storage-3 -> store, everyone downstream of the file kernel: **the decision exposed a gap in the KERNEL, not in the byte model.** `fn-sf-crash-imagep` (`books/store-files.lisp:593`) has no freedom for a record that recovery replayed from the view and has not yet re-fenced, so a crash in the recovery window produces an image it does not admit -- and that window is the campaign's ordinary path (`tests/campaign` kills at `record-linked` and reopens). Nothing acknowledged is at risk there: such a state carries no success, because `fn-sn-initial nil 0` starts with none and `Store.recover` runs exactly once per process, at open (`run_store.py:1674`, `run_owner.py:660`, `fn9p.py:428`, `run_reader.py:313`, `run_bp_ingress.py:132`). So K2 now takes `(not (fn-bs-replay-visiblep ks))` as a hypothesis and the window is the new open row **K2r** in §3.3/§7, whose content is that carried emptiness. **Widening `fn-sf-crash-imagep` was NOT taken and is the cross-cluster proposal**: it changes the premise `books/store-observed.lisp` and the whole store-node closure take from the kernel, and it is not one lane's to make silently. Recorded on `FLR-001`.

CHANGE w9/storage-3 -> store, byte-store (M2): **`books/byte-store-scan` CERTIFIES** (hbox, evidence `build/acl2/certify-20260920T200057Z-1137489`, ACL2 8.7 under `swarm-build`; commit `1d3473d`). The ld/certify gap was FOUR concrete defects and none of the three hints already in the book was needed. What an `ld` driver inherits and a `certify-book` world does not is enabled RULES: (1) `fn-bs-member-of-append` is byte-store-invariants' own rule, withdrawn with `fn-bs-invariants-vocabulary` at that book's end -- without it `fn-bs-crash-select-names-are-an-outcome` recursed to the induction-depth-limit at 33,381,159 prover steps (`certify-20260920T193919Z-1122107`, log:16128); it is now ENABLED, not restated. (2) `len` of `append` is `local` in that book, so it is restated locally here, for `fn-bs-txn-names-length`. (3) **`fn-bs-assoc-of-name-in-entries` was FALSE as stated** -- the same shape as `fn-cpp-find-of-append`: `(strip-cars '(nil))` is `(nil)`, `nil` is a member of it, and `(assoc-equal nil '(nil))` is `nil`; it now hypothesises `(alistp alist)`. (4) `fn-bs-txn-name-not-in-txn-names` carried `:in-theory (disable fn-bs-txn-name)` on a CONSTRAINED function, which is a HARD ERROR under `certify-book` (no definition rule of that name) and silently fine under the probe's world. **Two general traps for other lanes**: a theory expression naming a constrained function is a certify-only hard error, and an `ld` probe that includes several books sees their withdrawn vocabularies.

NOTE w6/peering-feed-4 -> all: **one statement of the eleven was FALSE and is repaired, not weakened.** `fn-feed-done-is-never-selected` now carries `(fn-feed-selection f obs)` as a hypothesis. Without it, take `msgid` = `NIL` and a feed that selects nothing (no connection, say): `fn-feed-state-of` answers `NIL`, which is not `:queued`, and the selection IS `NIL`, so the conclusion `(not (equal (fn-feed-selection f obs) msgid))` fails -- the prover's own `Subgoal 73'`. The hypothesis holds on exactly the states where the host emits a command; `fn-feed-tick-step-offers-the-selection` already carried it and `fn-feed-tick-step-is-silent-without-a-selection` is the other half, so K5's "a finished entry is never offered again" is unchanged. One concrete violating value is in `tests/acl2/peer-feed-tests.lisp`. Three mechanisms closed the other ten, and all three are the same disease: a rule stated in one vocabulary and a goal carried in another. (1) The `-preserves-peer` family, one UNCONDITIONAL equation per transition and per field update -- the dispatcher keeps every arm closed, so each arm needs its own rewrite. (2) `(:d fn-feed-state-of)` and the four offer-state predicates CLOSED at every form from the folds onward: open, the goal reaches `fn-feed-entry-state` of `fn-feed-find` and not one queue lemma matches (`Subgoal 6'`, `Subgoal 103.59''`). (3) A `fn-feed-droppedp` propagation family stated with NO disequality, so the arms need no case split. Two lemmas were restated over a journal ENTRY rather than a loose `(kind values)` pair, because over a pair their conclusions have `f` and `msgid` free and ACL2 says so (`Warning [Free]`); same content, the fold's language.

## 2026-09-20 w10/substrate-2

CHANGE w10/substrate-2: **the four red substrate roots are CERTIFIED** — `books/stx-index`, `books/stx-epochs`, `books/stx-authority` and `tests/acl2/stx-transit-tests` — persvati, ACL2 8.7, `run-20260920T211342Z-aa20`, evidence `build/acl2/certify-20260920T211346Z-3327395`, **31 of 31 roots, exit code 0**, 128 s at `--jobs 4`, branch `w10/substrate-2`. Baseline for comparison, same box and command before any edit: `run-20260920T204626Z-b9b3`, 27 of 31. **No theorem statement was weakened and none was removed.** `fn-stx-commit-decode-is-a-commit` needed the result algebra of `books/statement-invariants` (`fn-stmt-okp-of-ok`, `fn-stmt-value-of-ok`) enabled at the one form — the checkpoint was `(TRUE-LISTP (FN-STMT-VALUE (FN-STMT-OK (LIST :FN-ME-COMMIT ...))))`, i.e. the commit was built and the conclusion could not see it — and NOT a shape lemma about `fn-stmt-decode-items`, which stays disabled. `fn-stx-index-equivocators-agree` needed the equality `(cdr (fn-stx-alist-get (fn-stx-slot-key s) (fn-stx-index-slots index)))` = `(fn-stx-lace-slot-first lace (fn-stx-slot-key s))`, which `fn-stx-index-slots-agree` states but could not deliver: its first conjunct is over `fn-stx-index-slot-first`, a term the goal never contained, and its second is an `iff` that cannot rewrite inside `(consp ...)`. It is now one step over one more statement plus two one-literal bridges, 1,778 prover steps and 0.01 s against 108,917 steps failing.

NOTE w10/substrate-2 -> everyone: **the fixed `tools/proof_profile.py` paid for itself on the first form.** Profiling `fn-stx-index-equivocators-agree` before any hint named fifteen runes with ZERO useful applications: `fn-stx-index-stmt-is-consp` 18,651 frames, `(:type-prescription fn-stmt-p)` 8,540, `(:definition fn-lace-p)` 6,996, `(:definition fn-stx-alist-get)` 5,880, `fn-prin-acceptablep-implies-shapes` 5,036, `fn-lace-member-is-stmt` 4,270, `fn-stx-lace-slot-first-of-member` 3,668. Every one is a shape rule that backchains on each list-shaped subterm the induction produces. They are withdrawn together as one local `deftheory`. The upstream cause is `books/stx-index.lisp:23`, a book-wide (local) enable of `(:d fn-lace-same-slotp)`, `(:d fn-lace-slot-conflictp)` and `(:d fn-lace-equivocatorp)`: every theorem below it that must not see them disables them in its own hint, which is the inversion `docs/proof-style.md` warns about. Narrowing it to the forms that want it is a clean follow-up.

NOTE w10/substrate-2 -> whoever reads S4-1 or S5-1: **those witnesses were VACUOUS and had never executed.** A statement whose kind is not `:article` carries its payload as the article body in base64 (`fn-stx-payload-for` / `fn-stx-body-payload`, `books/stx-carrier.lisp`), and both carrier articles in `tests/acl2/stx-transit-tests.lisp` were built with the prose body "p". So the reconstructed statement carried the wrong payload, no signature verified, `fn-stx-delta` was `nil`, and every assertion about the hostile batch held of an EMPTY delta. The tooth that caught it is the non-degeneracy line the previous lane wrote for exactly this purpose. Both carriers now carry `fn-stx-b64-encode` of their own signed payload, and two new assertions pin the deltas themselves so it cannot return silently. Three more forms below the two open theorems had never been admitted by anything and were wrong: `fn-stx-commits-of-lace-have-witnesses` was FALSE (it asked `fn-lace-p` of the delta where the scan needs `fn-prin-verifiedp` of its members; repaired with the hypothesis that holds on every reachable delta, not weakened), `fn-stx-every-commit-by-sublist`'s `:use` sat on the wrong subgoal of a three-way scheme, and **`fn-stx-index-lookup-cost-is-index-bounded` — the D3 cost shadow — suggested no induction scheme and had never been proved**; it is now the instance of a walk lemma with its statement unchanged. **General lesson: a red root hides every form below it, and "stated and committed" is not "admitted".**

ASK w10/substrate-2 -> whoever owns `books/node.lisp` and the served path (blocking for PRF-023 and PRF-025, not for certification): **neither substrate keystone has a host line, so both stay `in-progress`.** `fn-stx-index-lookup` and `fn-stx-index-equivocatorp` have no caller anywhere: `fn-node-statep` is `(acceptance retention stage bindings)` and has no index slot, so D3's "the served query walks the index, never the store" is proved about a query the served path does not carry. `fn-stx-commits-of-batch` likewise has no caller on any reconnection path. The interface change is the one the provenance lane also wants: a structured value carried beside the node, as `fn-stx-index` is designed to be — a fifth slot on `fn-node-statep` holding `(fn-stx-index-of-store (fn-stx-store node) keyring)`, with `fn-stx-index-invariant-preserved-by-accept` (already proved and certified) as the preservation obligation at the one transition that extends the store. Both `pending_subject` notes are in `planning/proof-events.json`.
